3.1 **The product is the Godot 4 game on Ryan's Windows PC only; the browser game is frozen as the rules oracle** (changed only
for rules fixes, so the parity check has something true to compare against). Why: "Godot seems way better. It's very
noticeable now." (R32); PC-only (Oct 24). Set: Ryan. Ids: R32, R33a, agents-02.

3.2 **Heavy work runs on Ryan's PC (RTX 5080) through the queue; the cloud sandbox is for editing.** Why: the sandbox has about
8 GB and no GPU and runs out of memory on full meshes. Set: Ryan, Oct 21. Id: agents-23.

3.3 **Nothing on Ryan's PC is deleted except a job's own leftovers.** A job may remove the temp copies and scratch folders it created, and old kept workspace copies that Git shows hold only rebuildable files, once its work is pushed. Everything else stays until Ryan says yes. Keep every job's PC folder lean, and the Board lists the PC folder sizes at each disk check.
Why: the PC disk filled on Nov 11 (C: 0.0 GB free, 550 GB in voidsworn_test, mostly old run copies), the queue
watcher stopped and every PC job waited until Ryan freed space himself (LOOP.md, Nov 12 02:15 and 02:20 UTC). On Nov 18 the drive fell under the 60 GB job floor from about 45 kept workspace copies and 390 GB of job scratch in the temp folder; Ryan chose (Nov 18 20:46 UTC) to let jobs clean up after themselves (R326).
Set: Ryan, Nov 18 (R326); lean folders and the disk check Board, Nov 11. Ids: ships-10, loop-17.

Before setting aside an earlier checkout, the runner reuses it only when its working tree is clean and all local history
(including reflogs) is in the freshly fetched mirror. It allows ignored Godot `.godot` and `.import` caches, Python
`__pycache__` and `.pyc` caches, and ignored files under `godot/assets/ships/` whose size and SHA-256 match the same path
in the shared build (issue 1107). It fetches and switches to the new branch with those files in place. A collision or an
uncertain check keeps the earlier checkout beside repo. More than two `repo-kept-*` copies triggers a warning naming each
copy and its size; older copies are removed only as the next paragraph allows. Why: repeated full copies filled C:, and deleting a supposedly clean checkout could lose
unique ignored work (issue 860, run 2, Board Nov 16).
A checkout that changes while the runner starts is never reset; the job stops instead.

Parity removes its own default temp folder when it finishes, pass or fail. After a checked push the runner removes
the scratch folder it recorded for that run. It keeps the newest `repo-kept-*` copy in the board folder and removes
older ones only when the same rebuildable-file and Git history checks pass. It logs each decision. A pause removes nothing (3.11).

3.4 **GPU work is sized to the job and to what the RTX 5080 can hold, not to a fixed job count; frame-time measurements run
solo. Lanes C and D stay code only.** The Board decides per job how many share the card: light captures and probes can run side by
side, heavy bakes and Blender renders take more of it, and nothing is started that would run the card out of its 16 GB of
graphics memory. The launchers take and release claims themselves through tools/pc/gpu_slots.js (shared capacity from
Downloads\voidsworn_test\gpu_slots.json, {"sharedSlots":2} by default, raised by the Board; captures weigh 1, Blender and heavy
bakes 2, with PC_GPU_WEIGHT for the job's chosen weight), or a solo claim that waits for every slot
to empty and blocks new GPU work while it measures. Dead process claims are reclaimed. `gpu_go.txt` still wins: no new GPU
work starts unless its first line is `GPU cleared` (optionally followed by `: reason`); the gaming pause in 3.11 is
unchanged. Why: "that one GPU job at a time is an outdated rule, we should be flexible based on the task and what my
graphicsa card can actually handle as a 5080. we don't want to overwhelm it but we don't want to artificially restrict small
jobs and extend things out" (Ryan, Nov 16, R293). Solo measurements keep speed numbers honest. Replaces the two-job cap
(R276, Nov 15) and the one-job limit before it (R173b, effects-09); the gaming pause (R173) remains. Set: Ryan, Nov 16 (R293).

3.5 **Every PC queue job is wrapped in the tmo.bat watchdog, ends with exit /b, and carries no |, &, < or > in its
arguments.** Agent runs stop after STALL_MIN minutes without progress (default 30), or at a hard ceiling (default six
hours; CODEX_CAP and WORK_CAP override the ceiling in seconds). Progress is a changed file write time in the run repo,
a new commit, or new bytes in log.txt (also stderr for workhorse runs). Git housekeeping does not count. A stall or ceiling
stop writes its reason, changed files and last log lines to the out folder's resume.txt and the -partial commit message;
the next run reads that note. timeouts.log records STALLED after N min or CEILING. Short queue commands and runner checks
keep their explicit time limits. Why: hung Godot windows blocked the queue for about three hours (G-3), but the old two-hour
agent cap killed jobs that were still working (367 and 371). Ryan, Nov 15: "do we need to get rid of that random 2 hour rule
if it's timing out actual work?" He took up a stall check, a high backstop and a saved resume note (issue 818). Set: Board
(G-3), Ryan (issue 818). Ids: G-3, feel-06, skill-pb-05.

## What this recording adds

The [F1 trace](../f1/0354234/README.md) showed the move ghost, target odds, player handover and arriving reinforcements.
The [look bench](../look_bench/README.md) showed isolated weapons, shields, deaths and hulls at fixed cameras.
The player path shows when fresh-run cards appear, where the danger hatching falls alongside move and target options,
and how the default cut-ins and story lines interrupt or frame those same decisions in a live campaign.

## Review notes

- Hullbreakers route, Battle 2 at 01:38. Tab was needed to restore the selected ship after an activation.
- Hullbreakers route, Battle 3 at 00:45. The target hover card stays over the letterboxed lance shot.
- Navy route, Battle 2 at 01:21. The reinforcement banner and the line naming the arriving ship overlap.
- The Windows export falls back to its older rocks when the scanned rock check looks for the raw GLB inside the pack.

## Rerun

Open `tools\pc\jobs\player_path.bat` with a shared installed build that includes the recorder. The launcher runs both
seeds and prints the elapsed time. The two successful captures and encodes took about 19 minutes on this PC, plus any
queue wait or startup retry. See the [recording notes](../../../contrib/codex/player_path_1119/NOTES.md) for optional
paths to the branch install and output folders.

-3.3 **Nothing on Ryan's PC is deleted except a job's own leftovers.** A job may remove the temp copies and scratch folders it created, and old kept workspace copies that Git shows hold only rebuildable files, once its work is pushed. Everything else stays until Ryan says yes. Keep every job's PC folder lean, and the Board lists the PC folder sizes at each disk check.
-Why: the PC disk filled on Nov 11 (C: 0.0 GB free, 550 GB in voidsworn_test, mostly old run copies), the queue
-watcher stopped and every PC job waited until Ryan freed space himself (LOOP.md, Nov 12 02:15 and 02:20 UTC). On Nov 18 the drive fell under the 60 GB job floor from about 45 kept workspace copies and 390 GB of job scratch in the temp folder; Ryan chose (Nov 18 20:46 UTC) to let jobs clean up after themselves (R326).
-Set: Ryan, Nov 18 (R326); lean folders and the disk check Board, Nov 11. Ids: ships-10, loop-17.
-
-Before setting aside an earlier checkout, the runner reuses it only when its working tree is clean and all local history
-(including reflogs) is in the freshly fetched mirror. It allows ignored Godot `.godot` and `.import` caches, Python
-`__pycache__` and `.pyc` caches, and ignored files under `godot/assets/ships/` whose size and SHA-256 match the same path
-in the shared build (issue 1107). It fetches and switches to the new branch with those files in place. A collision or an
-uncertain check keeps the earlier checkout beside repo. More than two `repo-kept-*` copies triggers a warning naming each
-copy and its size; older copies are removed only as the next paragraph allows. Why: repeated full copies filled C:, and deleting a supposedly clean checkout could lose
-unique ignored work (issue 860, run 2, Board Nov 16).
-A checkout that changes while the runner starts is never reset; the job stops instead.
-
-Parity removes its own default temp folder when it finishes, pass or fail. After a checked push the runner removes
-the scratch folder it recorded for that run. It keeps the newest `repo-kept-*` copy in the board folder and removes
-older ones only when the same rebuildable-file and Git history checks pass. It logs each decision. A pause removes nothing (3.11).
-
-3.4 **GPU work is sized to the job and to what the RTX 5080 can hold, not to a fixed job count; frame-time measurements run
-solo. Lanes C and D stay code only.** The Board decides per job how many share the card: light captures and probes can run side by
-side, heavy bakes and Blender renders take more of it, and nothing is started that would run the card out of its 16 GB of
-graphics memory. The launchers take and release claims themselves through tools/pc/gpu_slots.js (shared capacity from
-Downloads\voidsworn_test\gpu_slots.json, {"sharedSlots":2} by default, raised by the Board; captures weigh 1, Blender and heavy
-bakes 2, with PC_GPU_WEIGHT for the job's chosen weight), or a solo claim that waits for every slot
-to empty and blocks new GPU work while it measures. Dead process claims are reclaimed. `gpu_go.txt` still wins: no new GPU
-work starts unless its first line is `GPU cleared` (optionally followed by `: reason`); the gaming pause in 3.11 is
-unchanged. Why: "that one GPU job at a time is an outdated rule, we should be flexible based on the task and what my
-graphicsa card can actually handle as a 5080. we don't want to overwhelm it but we don't want to artificially restrict small
-jobs and extend things out" (Ryan, Nov 16, R293). Solo measurements keep speed numbers honest. Replaces the two-job cap
-(R276, Nov 15) and the one-job limit before it (R173b, effects-09); the gaming pause (R173) remains. Set: Ryan, Nov 16 (R293).
-
-3.5 **Every PC queue job is wrapped in the tmo.bat watchdog, ends with exit /b, and carries no |, &, < or > in its
-arguments.** Agent runs stop after STALL_MIN minutes without progress (default 30), or at a hard ceiling (default six
-hours; CODEX_CAP and WORK_CAP override the ceiling in seconds). Progress is a changed file write time in the run repo,
-a new commit, or new bytes in log.txt (also stderr for workhorse runs). Git housekeeping does not count. A stall or ceiling
-stop writes its reason, changed files and last log lines to the out folder's resume.txt and the -partial commit message;
-the next run reads that note. timeouts.log records STALLED after N min or CEILING. Short queue commands and runner checks
-keep their explicit time limits. Why: hung Godot windows blocked the queue for about three hours (G-3), but the old two-hour
-agent cap killed jobs that were still working (367 and 371). Ryan, Nov 15: "do we need to get rid of that random 2 hour rule
-if it's timing out actual work?" He took up a stall check, a high backstop and a saved resume note (issue 818). Set: Board
-(G-3), Ryan (issue 818). Ids: G-3, feel-06, skill-pb-05.
-
-3.6 **The Board may double-click .bat files on the PC itself by computer use, a few clicks at a time, releasing the lock at
-once, never while Ryan is playing; it never drives the PC by remote clicks to unstick a job.** Why: "can't you use computer
-use for all of this?" (R126); holding the lock hides his windows; Task Manager ignores remote clicks. Set: Ryan (R126); Board
-(the limits). Ids: R126a, R126b, loop-16, skill-pb-06.
-
-3.7 **Clone on the PC with `git -c core.autocrlf=false`.** Why: CRLF breaks the campaign table export. Set: Board. Id: loop-14.
-
-3.8 **Free tools and sources, local first; a paid service only with Ryan's yes, for that one evaluation.** Why: money is his.
-Set: Ryan (AGENTS.md; R110). Ids: R110b, agents-20, audit-02.
-
-3.9 **Performance has one binding law: the measured scene gate, 12.5 ms of the 16.7 ms frame on the #207 scene** (frame median
-at 1080p, GPU ms at 4K). Triangles, draw calls and surfaces are reported, never capped. 60 fps at 4K is the goal, 1440p the
-floor. Why: new content needs a spending limit, Ryan plays at 1440p and 4K (R116); "measurements exist to find where fidelity
-can scale up and where performance forces scale down" (R121). Set: Ryan, Nov 6 to 8 (R111, R113, R116, R121, R122a). Ids:
-R111a, R111b, R113, R116, R121a, R121b.
-
-3.10 **The repo stays public.** Why: the outside tools (Muse and its models) read it without logins (R216). Set: Ryan (R10,
-R216).
-
-3.11 **A pause never loses or deletes work.** When Ryan wants the PC (GPU or CPU), the Board runs tools/pc/pause_all.bat
-(now, or at a time): it withdraws the GPU go, closes only chain windows still waiting to start, and ends the Board's own AI
-processes (only those whose command line names the voidsworn_test job folders; never Ryan's own apps), so
-each running job's runner commits and pushes its work to the issue's -partial branch; job windows mid-run are never closed and
-nothing is deleted. The pause waits for each lane's runner (main, B, C, D and DeepSeek work217) to leave running, then checks
-its status.json, run_commit.txt and confirmed_commit.txt against GitHub with git ls-remote. pc_pause.txt reports each job as
-done (finished and its delivery branch holds its confirmed commit), checkpoint verified (its -partial branch holds the
-runner's confirmed commit), or unresolved (anything else, including checks still running, a failed verification or a timeout).
-Leftover running records are listed with their last activity, not counted in the wait or unresolved jobs, and never changed.
-Only the final "safe to restart" line in pc_pause.txt means a restart is safe; any unresolved job instead gives
-"NOT safe to restart: <jobs>". On Ryan's go the Board restarts every paused job from its verified -partial branch and recovers
-unresolved work before restarting the PC. Why: "I don't want to cause anything to get lost or deleted with the pauses as well,
-so let's bake that in" (R272); quiet agent processes did not prove a runner in final checks had pushed (R309, issue 944).
-Set: Ryan, Nov 15; audit fixes approved Nov 17 (R309).

-## 4. Ships and concepts
-
-4.1 **Space ships, never sea ships, in the whole shape.** No boat profile, keel, raked prow, deck house, mast, funnel, crane,
-railings, porthole rows or anything hanging off; a worked belly. Why: Ryan, Oct 22: "careful that these don't stretch into
-obvious naval-looking ships. again, these are SPACE ships"; Nov 11: "I don't understand the random things hanging off, it reads
-as naval. We need to do a full pass across all ships and factions so not naval" (R185). Set: Ryan (Oct 22, Oct 24, R185). Ids:
-agents-19, agents-25, R8b, R185a, concepts-16. Detail: docs/playbooks/concepts_round2.md gate line 11.
-
-4.2 **No gun faces backward or into the ship at rest; a turret may turn aft only to follow a target its rules arc lets it hit
-(6.16, R222).** Why: "no ship in the game fires backward" (Oct 22). Set: Ryan (Oct 22; the turret line Nov 12, R222).
-Ids: agents-17, blob-03, R222.
-
-4.3 **Parts look like they belong: snapped to the real hull on a visible mount, in matched pairs at sensible hard points; the
-Hullbreakers may be junky.** Why: bolt-ons on the AI hulls floated, sank or sat anywhere (Oct 21). Set: Ryan, Oct 21. Ids:
-agents-18, agents-24, factions-02.
-
-4.4 **A ship reads as armed from above at thumbnail size; each faction mounts its forward weapon its own way, never a long
-barrel on the nose tip; no two factions share an outline from above; identity shows under the battle camera.** Why: "It's
-really hard to tell if there's any guns right now so it barely looks like a warship" (R176); "same giant gun directly on nose.
-It's good to have forward facing but it always looks the same across factions" (R183). Set: Ryan's complaints; wording Board
-(R176, R183). Ids: R176b, R183a, R183b, concepts-04, concepts-05.
-
-4.5 **Every faction is its own: its own parts kit and shape language; only invisible plumbing is shared.** Why: "all the
-factions are supposed to be unique and different. Having the same parts everywhere is against that" (R151). Set: Ryan. Ids:
-R151b. The faction sheets (shape, colour, where guns sit, how it breaks, its Never list) are docs/playbooks/factions.md.
-
-4.6 **One concept style for every faction: realistic and detailed, like a photograph of a real built object**
-(contrib/muse/design/concept_directions_2026_11/corsair_A.jpg); a picture that reads as an illustration, a toy or a cartoon
-fails. Ryan judges the style, side by side; the Board never declares it. Why: "Pretty realistic and detailed to help with the
-builds... this style is how it should look in game" (R197); "It's semi realistic but not quite" (R198). Set: Ryan, Nov 11.
-
-4.7 **When Ryan likes a picture, the next image job edits that picture**; colour alone never sends a picture back; when image
-rounds cannot reach the style without breaking the shape, stop and use the Opus build-sheet route. Why: six Hullbreaker rounds
-were lost redescribing a liked picture in words (R192); "we can fix color ourselves rather than get stuck in a loop with color
-fixes with image generation" (R204). Set: Ryan and Board, Nov 11 (R192, R204, R206).
-
-4.8 **No generated concept reaches a build, or Ryan, without the Board's review against the written gate**
-(docs/playbooks/concepts_round2.md). The gate's ratios and fractions (proportions per class, the stern fraction) are starting
-points for a drawing, not identity. Why: "I need you to do a much more in depth review and refinement before these ever go to
-actual builds" (R172); Hegemony's 4.5 : 1 was overruled by R183's 3 : 1. Set: Ryan (R172); the ratio note Board. Ids: R172a,
-R172b, concepts-08, concepts-14.
-
-4.9 **Start from what was already liked.** Why: two rounds were lost designing the Glasswrights and Hullbreakers afresh and
-sanding off what made them theirs (R180). Set: Board lesson after Ryan's review (R180). Ids: R180c, factions-17.
-
-4.10 **The finished battle is Sunward Crusade against Eclipse, on the dead-star board.** Why: one finished fight proves every
-recipe and is the reference for later jobs (R167: "I want this to end with something that opus or other models can run
-from"). Set: Ryan (R167, R180). Ids: R167b, R180d, handoff-01.
-
-4.11 **Every part has a job and every weapon is its faction's own.** A part with no purpose is cut; weapons, turrets and
-launchers are designed per faction from the lore bible and the faction sheet (how the weapon works shows in its mount,
-housing, barrel and muzzle), never a shared silhouette; a gun in another weapon's firing lane or backblast is a clash. Why:
-Ryan on the Hullbreakers: "let's get rid of the chains randomly around the ship. There's no purpose" and "a gun directly
-behind the rocket pods on a16 ... seems like it conflicts"; on Eclipse: "their turrets look the same as hullbreakers" (R221).
-Set: Ryan, Nov 12 (R221). Parts carry the hull's detail density: triangles and edge features per square unit, per part
-type, within 25 percent of the hull's, reported on every sheet (R224: "They also just look smoother and less detailed, maybe
-slightly less poly than the rest of the ship").

-## 5. Ship build pipeline
-
-5.1 **No ship is built until its build sheet passes the concept standard** (contrib/muse/design/concept_standard_2026_11/standard.md:
-true side and top at one scale, underside, bow-on, length from game data, scale bar, a feature table that outranks the
-pictures). Why: "I want to make sure that they are good enough to actually hand build from in blender" (R203). Set: Ryan,
-Nov 11.
-
-5.2 **The concept guides the build; it is not traced.** The Opus builder is the designer of record: it keeps the picture's
-identity (mass, outline from above, character, palette, where the big features sit), fixes what would not work as a built
-object, takes guns from the hull's data.js slots as batteries that make sense, carries every tier and change of height the
-picture shows, and lists every change on the sheet. Why: "Use it to guide, not the very final exact design" (R207); "The guns
-look randomly placed and don't make sense... the builds look like square blocks basically" and "The concept should inform and
-guide but it's not a final play by play guide" (R213). Set: Ryan, Nov 11. Ids: R25 (concepts are inspiration), R207, R213.
-The Board (Opus) is the final designer and director; Muse concepts. Between an accepted concept and the build the Board makes
-the design calls, family distinctness included (a class that reads as a small copy of another class from above is changed in
-the build), lists them on the sheet and shows Ryan the result instead of asking first. Ryan's picks of concepts and his
-verdicts on pictures stand. Why: "You're the final designer and director. Muse is just concepting" (R233). Set: Ryan, Nov 12.
-
-5.3 **The settled build method is binding** (docs/playbooks/ships.md section 0; docs/ship_pipeline/RECIPE.md): the hull designed
-on the approved build sheet (5.1, 5.2), which the recipe builds as it is and never traces again (issue 592), with the stage 2
-AI shape reference made from the accepted concept as an outline and grime reference only, never shipped; hand-built parts, AI parts only through a reviewed faction
-library; paint from geometry masks, trim sheets and decals, never projected concept art; the seven stages. Why: projected
-concept paint was dropped on all 36 hulls (R82); Ryan adopted the research route (R96); the method kept drifting between
-documents (R214). Set: Ryan (R82, R96, R214, R215). Ids: R82d, R96a, R96c, R150a.
-
-5.4 **Breakable by design, with comprehensive insides.** Every hull has real thickness and insides through the whole hull
-(decks, frames, spaces, machinery; Chorus flesh and bone; Glasswright layered glass), rim bands and a dark liner where the
-intact ship shows; the build check fails a hull whose broken pieces show a hollow or flat inside. Hulls built before this rule
-rely on the game-side interior fallback (issue 167). Why: "the broken open pieces
-are visibly empty" (R98); "so we don't run into the issues of before with just empty looking husks" (R209); "I think it's
-better to be comprehensive with internal if we can without performance issues" (R215). Set: Ryan, Nov 4 and 11. Ids: R70,
-R98a, R98b, R99a, R209, R215.
-
-5.5 **Breaks follow the faction's rule and the class, never a fixed count or a straight cut**; hard points and breach anchors
-scale with class and faction, read from one table (factions.md; the check reads it, issue 363). Why: "is 28 hard points for
-every ship? It should vary by size of ship, faction, etc. We want things to be on theme." (R164); wrecks must look blown
-apart (R186). Set: Ryan (R164, R186, R215). R99 Q2 (two transverse lines plus one longitudinal) and AGENTS.md's "two or three
-break lines" are retired. Ids: R164d, R99b, R98b, ships-07, factions-04.
-
-5.6 **Hand-built parts carry the hull's level of surface detail.** Why: "the level of texture and details seem to vary wildly"
-(Oct 21). Set: Ryan. Id: agents-28.
-
-5.7 **Structure first: the massing carries the concept's variety of shape before any detail.** Why: "There's so much less
-variety in the shape and structure of it, so it's not just adding but almost removal." (R140). Set: Ryan, Nov 9. Ids: R140,
-R213.
-
-5.8 **The outline overlap against the concept is reported, not a pass bar** (was 90 percent, R147). Why: the builder is told
-to depart from the picture where it does not work (R207), and a 90 percent pass let a wrong bow through (R157). Set: Board,
-Nov 11 (reworded). Ids: R147, ships-05, recipe-03.
-
-5.9 **The blob sweep (nine cells: bow, midship, stern from top, side and below) is a viewing instrument for Ryan's eye, not a
-pass bar.** Why: assumed gates stop binding until measured (R121). Set: Ryan (R121d). Ids: agents-27, ships-08, blob-01.
-
-5.10 **The guns are never bent to fit a score.** Why: casemate barrels had been bent to raise the outline match (R156). Set:
-Ryan ("I agree with all of your takes", R156). Id: R156a.
-
-5.11 **Every recipe stage is one double-click, stops on its own failure with a non-zero exit, deletes nothing, and stage 7
-copies aside what it replaces before copying into the game.** Why: Ryan is not a coder; missing things fail loudly (R158).
-Set: Board and Muse's conditions (R158). Ids: recipe-01, recipe-02, recipe-08.
-
-5.12 **Ship assets ship as glb copied into the PC build; .vsm belongs to the frozen browser game.** Why: Godot cannot load
-.vsm. Set: Board (G-9, G-11).
-
-5.13 **Assets only under CC0 (Kenney, KayKit, Poly Haven, ambientCG); Quaternius is out.** Why: its licence is not CC0 (D11).
-Set: Board. Ids: D11, X14.
-
-5.14 **A build pull request carries its done-when evidence and a method line** (build sheet link, manifest, budget numbers,
-changes made to the sheet); the outside check returns one without them as blocking. Why: R214. Set: Ryan, Nov 11.
-
-5.15 **Parts read as what they are and fit together: the fit check.** Engines are bells with throats and rings, guns have a base,
-mantlet, breech and muzzle, launch tubes have depth; the bow and big masses carry the concept's shaping. No part inside
-another, in another gun's arc, in an exhaust cone or blocking a launcher; a fit view and fit report on every sheet and build.
-Why: round 2 sheets had guns blocking rockets and in front of an engine, tubes as flat circles, engines and guns as plain
-tubes, and a flat-block bow (Ryan, Nov 11, R217). Set: Ryan, Nov 11 (R217). Ids: ships-2c.
-
-5.16 **Fidelity is whole-ship: a parts inventory, one detail level, kit first, region by region.** Every feature the concept
-shows is listed with its sub-parts and modelled; every part sits at the same detail level as its neighbours; each faction's
-kit holds every part type at that level; the fit check covers every part; the sheet ticks the inventory per blob-sweep cell
-beside the concept. Feedback on one part becomes a rule for all parts. Why: "Engines and guns are the reference but we want
-to carry that through to the whole model. Don't just patch singular behavior or examples, use it for global learning and
-shaping the approach" (Ryan, Nov 11, R218). Set: Ryan, Nov 11 (R218). Ids: ships-2c.
-
-5.17 **A machine never crosses a seam; structure that crosses a seam is torn at it, insides included.** Guns, turrets,
-casemates, launchers, engines, thrusters, claws and sensor masts ride whole with one piece: the seam moves, or kinks round
-them in plan. Hull plating, frames, ribs, rails, openings such as a launch bay, and every inside (decks, bulkheads, rooms,
-the liner) tear across a seam, along the same ragged line as the plating over them: no inside panel stays whole across a
-break or sticks out past its piece's broken hull. The stage 4 audit reads each part's kind from the build (the faction kit's
-MACHINES) and fails only a machine across a seam; the export carries each seam's path and each machine's box, and the game's
-death cut reads them there (fx/test/death_seam_check.gd: zero machines split, zero insides outside their piece). Why: a cut
-gun reads as sliced, a torn plate as blown apart (R186, "blown apart, never sliced"); whole deck and bulkhead slabs stuck out
-of the broken A16 as big flat squares (Ryan, Nov 12, issue 600). Set: Board, Nov 12 (R226; insides added from Ryan's eye,
-issue 600). Ids: ships-2c.
-
-## 6. Battle look
-
-6.1 **Judge everything at the battle camera (camera 170, the radius Ryan plays at) at true pixel size, before any close-up.**
-Why: review sheets were made closer than the camera can go (R157); Ryan plays at the standard radius and zooms in for effects
-(R123). Set: Board (R157, R160); camera harness Ryan (R123c). Ids: R157a, program-02, skill-sv-01.
-
-6.2 **Comfort is protected for everyone.** At most three big flashes a second, "big" by the W3C guideline's combined area in a
-10 degree window, worked from a stated screen (default 32 inch at 24 inches); a reduce flashing option that keeps sizes and
-softens the rise and fall. Tune inside the counter; never quote a strictness figure without doing the arithmetic. Why: WCAG
-2.3.1 photosensitivity; Ryan: "Count by area" (R193). Set: Ryan (R187, R193); Board (R187b). Ids: R49d, R187b, uispec-04,
-plan-08.
-
-6.3 **Every explosion is route B: flash plus hot core.** Why: Ryan: "Far right in both for sure." (R170). Set: Ryan, Nov 10.
-
-6.4 **Flame flashes and snuffs out: no flame over 0.70 s, no hot core at full heat over 0.25 s; this cap is for flame only.**
-Sparks, debris, gas and cooling embers live longer, so hits throw things and leave marks. Why: fire looked wrong for space and
-deaths felt sudden (R88); the cap had been applied to everything, so nothing was thrown (R193: "Flame only"). Set: Ryan (R88,
-R158, R193). Ids: R88b, R158a, effects-03, deaths-02.
-
-6.5 **Wrecks look blown apart, never sliced**: uneven pieces, no straight line across the hull, a missing bite where the core
-went, ragged scorched rims, fragments between. A lance kill may stay cleaner along the beam. Why: "It's like straight lines and
-even halves. It doesn't feel like an explosion or major hit happened." (R186). Set: Ryan, Nov 11. Ids: R186, deaths-05,
-deaths-09, deaths-11.
-
-6.6 **The kill moment holds and slows on cruiser, capital and flagship kills** (the camera stays through the core flash, a
-short skippable slow beat). Why: "just do big ships and flagship for slow mo" (R73); "Hold and slow" (R193). Set: Ryan (R73,
-R193); replaces the Board's R123 "the camera is never taken for a capital death". Ids: R73a, R123a, deaths-04, uispec-05.
-
-6.7 **Effects start on the ship** (a hard point, a breach anchor or the hit surface), never at a box corner or in empty space.
-Why: "random thrusters or smoke popped up away from the ship itself? it looks all wrong" (R150). Set: Ryan (R150); method
-Board (R153). Built: the attach point service in godot/fx/fx_hull.gd (authored point, else surface ray, else skipped and
-counted), checked by godot/fx/test/attach_check.gd (issue 370). Ids: R150b, R153, effects-05.
-
-6.8 **Hits are weighty and readable at the battle camera**: mass-scaled shudder and kicks corrected by thrusters, damage on the
-hull itself with per-weapon marks, wounds that cool to scars, thrown matter big enough to read at camera 170; light on
-neighbouring hulls stays faint until the counter measures lit area. Why: "a simple cartoonish push back. It doesn't feel
-weighty, cinematic or accurate." (R50); "it's just an orange shape with weird smoke, the actual underlying ship looks the same"
-(R39); "Merge, then push harder" (R208). Set: Ryan (R39, R43, R50, R208). Ids: R39, R43, R50a, R208.
-
-6.9 **No flat lines for fire or hits**: weapon fire and hits are baked sims (flipbooks), sparks are small glowing points with
-curved, slowing trails. Why: "It looks so simple and like Microsoft paint." (R146). Set: Ryan, Nov 9. Ids: R146, R90a.
-
-6.10 **A bigger ship dies longer, not just larger; each faction breaks its own way; the flash core stays near white so a death
-always reads as a death.** Why: every faction died the same faint puff (deaths_and_hits.md). Set: Board, Nov 11, from Muse's
-staging table; the sizes and times are tuning values (0.4). Ids: deaths-03, deaths-10. Detail:
-docs/playbooks/deaths_and_hits.md.
-
-6.11 **Shields in space keep their faction colours; at rest, Rim and Gap: a faint rim in the faction edge colour on live
-sides, a dark gap where a side is open, nothing else** (R246, Nov 14). Why: his Oct 24 ruling (the cyan-only rule retired) and his
-Rim and Gap pick with Muse (R246). Set: Ryan (R3, R246). SUPERSEDED, kept so nobody follows it: "at rest invisible, a faint edge
-glow on zoom, hover or a recent hit" (R163, ended by R246). Ids: R3a, R163a, R246, effects-04.
-
-6.12 **Effects may spend the RTX 5080's headroom inside the scene gate, with a lower setting kept.** Set: Ryan (R88c). See
-3.9.
-
-6.13 **Plasma is light blue.** Why: his call on the damage marks reference (R46). Set: Ryan, Oct 26. Ids: R46a, lore-04.
-
-6.14 **Normal hits mark the hull (scorch, wounds, scars); a hull opens into a real break only when a section is nearly gone or
-on death.** 20 percent of a section's hull is the starting value, judged by eye. Why: cut-out holes on every hit "looked like
-hollow cardboard" (Nov 1 playtest). Set: Ryan (R80, R216).
-
-6.15 **Section damage tint may show on small ships if it reads at camera 170**; the hits pass tries it by eye on frigates (the
-old ban had no reason and is retired). Set: Ryan (R80, retired R216).
-
-6.16 **Spend death effect cost where the eye is (the tell, the break, the core flash); long cooling stays cheap.** 2 s and 14 s
-are starting values. Why: deaths now run 3.5 to over 10 seconds in three beats (R186, R193). Set: Ryan (R90, R216).
-
-6.17 **Turret guns turn to face their target when they fire; fixed side guns (casemates, broadside batteries) stay fixed.**
-A turret traverses smoothly (never snaps, RULEBOOK 7.1), within its arc, and returns to rest; the gun's muzzle and its effects
-start at the turned barrel. Why: today every gun is a fixed mesh (no aim code in godot/render/ship_view.gd or
-godot/fx/fx_weapons.gd); turning turrets make a fight read and suit the detailed guns now being built (R217). Set: Ryan, Nov 11
-(R219: "I agree with your design on turrets"). A turret may turn aft only to follow a target its rules arc lets it hit
-there; at rest no gun ever points backward (4.2). Set: Ryan, Nov 12 (R222: "yes agree on your turret facing rule"). Built (issue 560): a turret is its own node in the ship's glb
-(docs/ship_pipeline/HARDPOINTS.md "Turrets"; the kit marks it, tools/blender/kits/README.md); the attach point service in
-godot/fx/fx_hull.gd turns it, checked by godot/render/test/turret_check.gd. Hulls exported before it keep fixed guns.

-## 7. Movement
-
-7.1 **Movement is never sudden, jerky or stop-start, and feels like space**: thrusters fire wherever a ship would need them
-(braking, turning, stopping a turn, sliding). Why: "I don't want things to look jerky and the smooth call was because movement
-was very stop/start and sudden. Thrusters should be involved when it makes sense on braking and turns." (R189). Set: Ryan,
-Nov 11. Replaces the letter of R76 ("thruster puffs only on turns, no side-to-side shuffle"). Ids: R76a, R189a, movespec-01.
-
-7.2 **The main engine does the travelling.** A one-hex sidestep or back-off may slide on thrusters; a longer run turns its nose
-onto the course and burns the main engine; anything flown on thrusters alone is visibly slower and gentler. Why: "for one tile
-over, it tracks but not when they're going like 3-10 tiles down just almost skating on thrusters" (R195). Set: Ryan, Nov 11.
-
-7.3 **One ship moves at a time, at a weighty pace (about twice as slow as Nov 1); longer enemy turns are accepted; cinematic is
-the default with a brisk option.** Why: "I don't want to sacrifice the feel of the game/space/the ships." (R50). Set: Ryan
-(R50, R64); option Board (R50d). Ids: R50b, R50d, R64a, movespec-06.
-
-7.4 **Movement playback never changes rules results.** A LOOK change keeps the route, end hex and facing; a RULE change (route
-or facing) needs Ryan's own ruling and touches both rule twins. Why: R188's frame for the audit. Set: Board, Nov 11 (R188).
-
-7.5 **No roll into turns.** Why: nothing in space leans a ship; the audit reversed the earlier bank (movement_audit_code.md).
-Set: Board, Nov 11. Id: movespec-04 (the 4 degree bank, retired).
-
-7.6 **Flip and burn stays off for now, to be looked at again now that braking jets are on screen.** Why: left alone on the
-Board's advice (R193). Set: Ryan (R64, R193). Ids: R64c, feel-02.

-7.7 **Every number in contrib/opus/briefs/movement_spec.md is a tuning value chosen by eye from motion strips.** Set: Board
-(R189's rewrite). Ids: R131a, R131b, movespec-02, movespec-03, movespec-05, movespec-07.

-7.8 **Strike craft move like small agile craft (quick turns, short burns), still never jerky.** Why: small craft should feel

-## 8. Battle sound
-
-8.1 **A battle sounds clean and tactical; never thin and weak, cheesy or arcade-like, harsh or annoying.** No zaps, pews,
-rising whooshes or stock explosions; weight in the low and low-mid with a soft top. Why: "The sounds that I've heard from the
-game so far are bad" (R163). Set: Ryan (R163, R171). Ids: R171b, program-13, sound-01, sound-03.

-8.2 **Weapons stay dry; big moments get weight**: deaths, capital hits and shield breaks get tails, sub-bass, a bed for the

-built as no tails, so a ten second death had a one second sound (audit item 9); Ryan said "Go, in my order" (R193). Set:
-Board's reword, Ryan's go (R187, R193). Id: sound-02.

-fired (audit item 8). Set: Ryan's go (R193); built in issue 506.

+---
+name: screenshot-verdict
+description: Use for visual and teaching changes in Embers of the Hollow Sun. Record Battles 1 to 3 on the shared installed build with the player defaults, then use the look bench for fixed-camera comparisons.
+---
+
+# Screenshot verdict: record the player path and compare the look bench
+
+The product is the Godot game in `godot/`. Acceptance is Ryan's eye plus automatic checks (FINDINGS R6). RULEBOOK 12.6
+makes the player-path recording the acceptance check. The bench (issue 351) compares the same moments and cameras across
+revisions (R157).
+
+## Player-path acceptance (R328)
+
+1. Run `tools/pc/jobs/player_path.bat` on Ryan's PC with the branch's shared installed build. Use both fresh Sunward runs
+   through Battles 1 to 3. The launcher uses the player's default settings and writes the full 1080p masters and the review copies.
+2. Watch both runs and judge the change in context. Report the moments where it shows up, including what the teaching cards,
+   target and danger overlays, cut-ins, and battle log are doing. Include the stamped clip and battle contact sheet for Ryan.
+3. Use the bench procedure below for matched cameras and effect timing. Ryan accepts visual and teaching changes from
+   the player-path recording.
+
+## The bench in one line
+
+Double-click `tools/pc/jobs/look_bench.bat` (copy it to `Downloads\voidsworn_test\` first, it runs from anywhere): it benches
+the newest main and compares it with the accepted baseline (`docs/shots/look_bench/BASELINE.txt`). For a branch:
+`look_bench.bat origin/<branch>` (after) and optionally a second commit (before). Details: `godot/bench/README.md`.
+
+What it draws, on a fixed seed and a frame-locked clock:
+- the moments sheet: every row of `godot/bench/look_moments.json` (each weapon on a hull and on a shield, shield hit, low and
+  break, every death class, one weapon on every weight, the eight PR 327 looks) at the three real cameras: 170 (the battle
+  camera, 1 to 1 pixels), 60 and 26 (the closest the game allows), as 30 frames a second strips;
+- the fleet sheet: all 36 hulls at the battle camera at true pixel size over the darkest sky, the brightest sky and a storm map;
+- before and after: every still side by side with one difference number (mean absolute difference, 0 to 255 levels).
+
+## Procedure
+
+1. Battle camera first (R157, R160 rule 4): judge at camera 170 at true pixel size before any close-up. 60 and 26 are for detail.
+   Never judge on a view closer than 26 (the effects gallery's free camera is a build check only).
+2. If the change needs a new moment, add a row to `godot/bench/look_moments.json` in the same pull request (no new capture script).
+3. Run the bench on the change and compare with main (or the baseline). PC job: the bench waits for the graphics card to be idle and
+   for the Board's go file on days the GPU is not already given (`look_bench.bat AFTER BEFORE go`).
+4. Read `summary.txt`: every still over the tolerance is listed by moment and camera. A change must differ where it is meant to and
+   nowhere else. A difference in a moment the change does not touch is a defect (or a new exempt still: say which and why).
+5. Look at the sheets yourself: the overview pages first (one key frame per moment at the three cameras), then the strips of every
+   moment that differs. List what differs and anything that looks wrong (blocks instead of ships, black squares, pale squares,
+   puffs in empty space, vanished dark hulls, burnt-out pale hulls, naval tells, backward guns).
+6. Put the before and after sheets for the moments that changed in the pull request (docs/shots/<job>/, JPG at sensible sizes) and
+   lead with camera 170. Commit a new baseline (sheets in docs/shots/look_bench/ and the commit in BASELINE.txt) only when the
+   Board accepts the change.
+7. Show Ryan the sheet: two lines and one image, with your pick.
+
+## Tolerance and exempt stills
+
+The tolerance and the stills that cannot repeat are measured, not guessed: see `docs/shots/look_bench/README.md` (two runs of the
+same commit). Wreck physics is frame-locked in the bench (`--fixed-fps 60`, one effect step per drawn frame), so deaths repeat there
+even though the game runs them on real time (R118).
+
+## Stops and traps
+
+- `placeholder hull`: the bench stops if any ship would be drawn as a block (a checkout without `godot/gen`). Copy the PC-built
+  hulls in (`LB_SHIPS`, LOOP.md Known traps) and run again. Never show or judge a sheet with blocks (issue 281 run 6).
+- The bench draws no hex grid and no HUD. For the whole battle scene use `godot/bench/scene_gate.tscn -- --still`.
+- `render/ship_view.gd` runs its idle bob and station-keeping jets on the wall clock; the bench parks ShipView processing so ships hold
+  still. A change to those two looks cannot be judged on the bench until they move to the effect clock.
+- Effects that are fine in one renderer can draw black squares on the real GPU (NaN from pow with a negative base or a zero-length
+  normalize, spread by bloom): guard shaders with `pow(max(b, 0.0), e)`, a safe normalize and clamped outputs.
+- The frozen browser build's tools (closeshot.py, fxshot.py, boardshot.py) are history; the browser game is the rules oracle only.

godot\campaign\README.md:76:A fresh Godot save (browser saves are not carried over, R33): `user://voidsworn_campaign.json` plus a backup one save behind (`.bak`). Both are written atomically: whole to a `.tmp` file, flushed and size-checked, then renamed over the old file, so a crash mid-write leaves the old save or the new one, never half a file (`test/save_test.gd`). It holds the profile (`meta`), the saved run and a slim run log. Every non-integer number is written as its exact IEEE hex, because Godot's JSON reader can land one unit in the last place off. A damaged save falls back to the backup; the half that is sound is kept. Set `persist_on = true` and call `persist_load()` at start (the parity harness keeps saves in memory).

param(
  [string]$Install,
  [string]$Full,
  [string]$Review,
  [ValidateSet(10, 121)][int[]]$Seeds = @(121, 10)
)
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
$pc = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE 'Downloads\voidsworn_test'
$repo = Split-Path (Split-Path (Split-Path $PSScriptRoot))
if (-not $Install) { $Install = Join-Path $pc 'build\export' }
$stamp = Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd_HHmmss'
if (-not $Full) { $Full = Join-Path $pc "shots\player_path_$stamp" }
if (-not $Review) { $Review = Join-Path $Full 'review' }
$Install = [IO.Path]::GetFullPath($Install)
$Full = [IO.Path]::GetFullPath($Full)
$Review = [IO.Path]::GetFullPath($Review)
$game = Join-Path $Install 'Voidsworn.console.exe'
$pack = Join-Path $Install 'Voidsworn.pck'
foreach ($file in @($game, $pack)) {
  if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $file -PathType Leaf)) { throw "Missing installed game file: $file" }
}
if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Full) { throw "Choose a new full recording folder: $Full" }
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $Full | Out-Null
$go = Join-Path $pc 'gpu_go.txt'
$python = (Get-Content -LiteralPath (Join-Path $pc 'python_path.txt') -TotalCount 1).Trim()
$compose = Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'compose.py'
& $python $compose --check
if ($LASTEXITCODE) { throw 'The recording encoder did not pass its preflight' }

$watch = [Diagnostics.Stopwatch]::StartNew()
$buildHash = (Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 -LiteralPath $pack).Hash.ToLowerInvariant()
$temp = Join-Path $env:TEMP ('voidsworn-player-path-' + [guid]::NewGuid())
$tmo = Join-Path $repo 'tools\pc\tmo.bat'
$keepAppData = $env:APPDATA
$keepProfile = $env:VS_PLAYER_PATH_PROFILE
$keepMode = $env:PC_GPU_MODE
$keepWeight = $env:PC_GPU_WEIGHT
$failures = @()
try {
  foreach ($seed in $Seeds) {
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $go) -or (Get-Content -LiteralPath $go -TotalCount 1) -notmatch '^GPU cleared(?:$|:)') {
      throw "The GPU queue is paused. See $go"
    }
    $avi = Join-Path $Full "seed-$seed.avi"
    $trace = Join-Path $Full "seed-$seed.jsonl"
    foreach ($attempt in 1, 2) {
      $attemptPath = Join-Path $temp "seed-$seed-attempt-$attempt"
      $env:APPDATA = Join-Path $attemptPath 'profile'
      $env:VS_PLAYER_PATH_PROFILE = $env:APPDATA
      New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $env:APPDATA | Out-Null
      $env:PC_GPU_MODE = 'shared'
      $env:PC_GPU_WEIGHT = '1'
      $log = Join-Path $attemptPath 'launcher.log'
      $gameLog = Join-Path $attemptPath 'game.log'
      Write-Host "Recording seed $seed to $avi"
      $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
      try {
        & $tmo 1800 $game --log-file $gameLog --resolution 1920x1080 --windowed --disable-vsync --fixed-fps 30 --write-movie $avi -- --player-path $trace --player-path-seed $seed 2>&1 | Out-File -LiteralPath $log -Encoding utf8
        $code = $LASTEXITCODE
      } finally { $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop' }
      # The sector map builds in a background thread. A slow first load can miss the jump before Battle 1 starts.
      $startupTimeout = $code -and (Test-Path -LiteralPath $trace) -and
        (Select-String -LiteralPath $trace -Pattern '"battle":0.*"reason":"jump to 0-0 did not open"' -Quiet)
      if ($attempt -eq 2 -or -not $startupTimeout) { break }
      foreach ($file in @($avi, $trace)) {
        if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $file) { Move-Item -LiteralPath $file -Destination $attemptPath }
      }
      Write-Host "Retrying seed $seed after the first map jump timed out"
    }
    if ($code) {
      $failures += "Seed $seed failed. See $log and $gameLog"
    }
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $avi) -or -not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $trace)) {
      $failures += "Seed $seed produced no recording. Check the installed recorder and $log"
      continue
    }
    & $python $compose $Full $Review $temp $seed $buildHash
    if ($LASTEXITCODE) { $failures += "Seed $seed review encoding failed. Masters are in $Full" }
  }
} finally {
  $env:APPDATA = $keepAppData
  $env:VS_PLAYER_PATH_PROFILE = $keepProfile
  $env:PC_GPU_MODE = $keepMode
  $env:PC_GPU_WEIGHT = $keepWeight
}
if ((Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 -LiteralPath $pack).Hash.ToLowerInvariant() -ne $buildHash) {
  throw 'The installed build changed during the recording'
}
if ($failures.Count) { throw ($failures -join [Environment]::NewLine) }
"Full run: {0:N0} seconds" -f $watch.Elapsed.TotalSeconds | Tee-Object -FilePath (Join-Path $Full 'elapsed.txt')
Write-Host "Full 1080p recordings: $Full"
Write-Host "Review clips and sheets: $Review"

 succeeded in 1370ms:
# Player-path recorder

Run `tools\pc\jobs\player_path.bat` on the PC with a shared Windows install that includes the recorder. The launcher
uses `Downloads\voidsworn_test\build\export` by default. To use a particular branch's installed build and choose the output
folders, pass the export folder, an unused full recording folder, and the review folder in that order. The installed pack
needs the ordinary export's `res://build_info.json` revision metadata.
Pass seed `10` or `121` as the fourth argument to rerun just that route.
The launcher retries once if the first map jump times out before Battle 1.

The launcher uses the PC's Python with Pillow and FFmpeg. It finds FFmpeg through `FFMPEG`, the PATH, or the installed
`imageio_ffmpeg` package. It runs each seed with its own fresh app data folder. The game reads settings, teaching
history and saves through the same `user://` paths it always uses, so starting fresh gives the actual shipped defaults.
The recording window is 1920 by 1080. Godot Movie Maker records the normal viewport and audio at 30 frames per second.
The launcher runs the game through `tmo.bat`, one shared GPU slot per seed.

The driver opens New run, takes the normal map and reward actions, and uses the same battle controls as a player.
It reads reach, odds, objectives and the visible battle state to choose inputs. It records presentation cues on the
movie frame clock. The fresh-run teaching rules pin the first two opponents. The sector faction and third opponent
are Hegemony Navy with seed 10 and Ashfall Hullbreakers with seed 121. Both second battles use the relay and its
teaching reinforcement.

The build hash is SHA-256 of the installed `Voidsworn.pck`. The manifest hash is SHA-256 of the
`res://gen/manifest.json` read from inside that pack. Provenance includes the source commit, and a working-source
hash when supplied by the build. Contact sheets and clips use battle-relative time. The two 1080p AVI masters keep
the opening menu and the campaign screens between battles. The JSONL beside each master records the source frame
of each cue, including the start and release of each cut-in.
@echo off
rem Issue 359: the Godot checks that need a window, the GPU or the built ship files, on Ryan's PC. Double-click, or call it
rem from the root of a clone (made with core.autocrlf=false). It runs every "gpu" line of godot\tools\ci\godot_checks.txt
rem (GitHub runs the "headless" lines), one after another, each under tools\pc\tmo.bat with a 15 minute limit.
rem   tools\pc\jobs\godot_gpu_checks.bat [path to the Godot console exe]
rem Ship files: the probes that draw real ships need godot\gen in this clone (the copy-and-verify step). Without it they stop
rem at once on a placeholder hull (render\placeholder_guard.gd) and the results file says STOPPED for them.
rem Output: ..\shots\gpu_checks\<check>\ (its pictures and log.txt) and ONE results file, ..\shots\gpu_checks\results.txt:
rem one line per check (PASS, FAIL with the exit code, or STOPPED), then the totals.
rem Unrun when written (issue 359 was built in the cloud): the first PC run is its test.
setlocal EnableDelayedExpansion
set "ROOT=%~dp0..\..\"
pushd "%ROOT%"
set "ROOT=%CD%\"
set "G=%~1"
if not defined G set "G=%GODOT_EXE%"
if not defined G if exist "%ROOT%..\godot_path.txt" set /p G=<"%ROOT%..\godot_path.txt"
if not defined G if exist "%USERPROFILE%\Downloads\voidsworn_test\godot\Godot_v4.7.2-stable_win64_console.exe" set "G=%USERPROFILE%\Downloads\voidsworn_test\godot\Godot_v4.7.2-stable_win64_console.exe"
if not exist "%G%" (
  echo FAIL: Godot console exe not found. Pass its full path, or set GODOT_EXE.
  popd
  pause
  exit /b 1
)
set "OUT=%ROOT%..\shots\gpu_checks"
set "R=%OUT%\results.txt"
if not exist "%OUT%" mkdir "%OUT%"
rem user:// for these runs lives in the output folder, never in the player's profile
set "APPDATA=%OUT%\appdata"
set "LOCALAPPDATA=%OUT%\appdata"
if not exist "%APPDATA%" mkdir "%APPDATA%"
if not exist "%ROOT%.onboarding_checks" mkdir "%ROOT%.onboarding_checks"
echo GPU Godot checks (godot\tools\ci\godot_checks.txt, class gpu), %date% %time% > "%R%"
echo Clone: %ROOT% >> "%R%"
if exist "%ROOT%godot\gen" (echo Ship files: godot\gen is present >> "%R%") else (echo Ship files: godot\gen is MISSING, probes that draw real ships will stop >> "%R%")
for /f "usebackq eol=# tokens=1-4 delims=|" %%a in ("%ROOT%godot\tools\ci\godot_checks.txt") do (
  if "%%a"=="gpu" call :one "%%b" "%%c" "%%d"
)
set "NP=0"
set "NT=0"
for /f %%k in ('findstr /b /c:"PASS " "%R%" ^| find /c /v ""') do set "NP=%%k"
for /f %%k in ('findstr /b /c:"PASS " /c:"FAIL " /c:"STOPPED " "%R%" ^| find /c /v ""') do set "NT=%%k"
echo Totals: !NP! of !NT! GPU checks passed. >> "%R%"
type "%R%"
popd
exit /b 0

"""Picture labels and review evidence. Pillow is already used by the sheet tools.

New renders call stamp_file immediately after saving. Compositors use Review.open
and Review.save: input revisions survive crops, resizing and later sheet runs.
The covered pixels live in the sidecar so alpha measurements see the original.
"""
import argparse
import base64
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from functools import lru_cache
import hashlib
import io
import json
import shutil
from pathlib import Path
import re
import subprocess
import sys
from urllib.parse import quote, unquote, urlsplit

if __name__ != '__main__' or sys.argv[1:2] != ['context']:
    from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageSequence

STAGES = ('sheet built', 'paint proof', 'installed in game')
EXTENSIONS = {'.png', '.jpg', '.jpeg', '.webp', '.gif'}
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]


def digest(data):
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def sidecar(path):
    return Path(str(path) + '.provenance.json')


def git(repo, *args):
    return subprocess.check_output(['git', '-C', str(repo), *args], stderr=subprocess.PIPE)


@lru_cache(maxsize=8)
def revision(repo):
    commit = git(repo, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD').decode().strip()
    if not re.fullmatch(r'[a-f0-9]{40,64}', commit):
        raise ValueError('picture source has no full git commit')
    changes = git(repo, 'diff', '--binary', 'HEAD')
    # Include new source files as well as tracked edits. Do not hash PC assets.
    for name in git(repo, 'ls-files', '--others', '--exclude-standard', '-z').decode().split('\0'):
        if name and name != 'CODEX_TASK.md' and Path(name).suffix in {'.py', '.gd', '.js', '.ps1', '.bat'}:
            changes += name.encode() + b'\0' + (Path(repo) / name).read_bytes()
    return dict(source_commit=commit, source_dirty=bool(changes),
                source_diff_sha256=digest(changes) if changes else '')


def text_lines(record):
    lines = [record['picture_name']]
    for src in record['sources']:
        rev = src['source_commit'][:12]
        if 'source_commit_date' in src:
            rev = 'built from ' + rev
        elif src['source_dirty']:
            rev += ' + working ' + src['source_diff_sha256'][:8]
        line = rev + ' | ' + src['stage']
        if line not in lines:
            lines.append(line)
    lines.append(record['stage'] + ' | ' + record['date'])
    return lines


def strip_for(record, size):
    # One bitmap alphabet for Python and Godot, independent of installed fonts.
    glyphs = json.loads((Path(__file__).with_name('picture_font.json')).read_text())
    scale = 2 if size[0] >= 1000 else 1
    width = size[0] // scale
    lines = []
    for text in text_lines(record):
        while text:
            end = max(1, (width - 12) // 6)
            lines.append(text[:end])
            text = text[end:]
    height = len(lines) * 13 + 10
    if height * scale > size[1]:
        raise ValueError('picture is too small for a readable provenance strip')
    strip_width = min(width, max(len(t) * 6 for t in lines) + 12)
    strip = Image.new('RGB', (strip_width, height), '#10151e')
    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(strip)
    draw.line((0, 0, strip_width, 0), fill='#e8b56b')
    for row, text in enumerate(lines):
        for col, char in enumerate(text):
            for y, bits in enumerate(glyphs.get(char, glyphs['?'])):
                for x in range(6):
                    if bits & (1 << (5 - x)):
                        draw.point((6 + col * 6 + x, 4 + row * 13 + y), fill='#f1e8d9')
    return strip.resize((strip.width * scale, height * scale), Image.Resampling.NEAREST)


def summary(record):
    keys = ('picture_name', 'source_commit', 'source_dirty', 'source_diff_sha256',
            'stage', 'date', 'image_sha256', 'sources')
    result = {k: record[k] for k in keys}
    if 'source_commit_date' in record:
        result['source_commit_date'] = record['source_commit_date']
    return result


def verify(path):
    path = Path(path)
    record = json.loads(sidecar(path).read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    if record.get('schema') != 1 or record.get('picture_name') != path.name:
        raise ValueError('wrong picture name or provenance schema: ' + str(path))
    if record.get('stage') not in STAGES:
        raise ValueError('missing or invalid picture stage: ' + str(path))
    datetime.strptime(record['date'], '%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ')
    for src in [record] + record['sources']:
        if not re.fullmatch(r'[a-f0-9]{40,64}', src['source_commit']) or src['stage'] not in STAGES:
            raise ValueError('invalid source revision/stage: ' + str(path))
        if src['source_dirty'] and not re.fullmatch(r'[a-f0-9]{64}', src['source_diff_sha256']):
            raise ValueError('working revision lacks its digest: ' + str(path))
        if 'source_commit_date' in src:
            datetime.strptime(src['source_commit_date'], '%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ')
            if src['source_dirty'] or src['source_diff_sha256']:
                raise ValueError('build revision has a working-tree state: ' + str(path))
    if not record['sources'] or record['image_sha256'] != digest(path.read_bytes()):
        raise ValueError('picture changed after stamping: ' + str(path))
    with Image.open(path) as image:
        box = record['stamp_box']
        frames = [f.copy() for f in ImageSequence.Iterator(image)]
        if len(frames) != len(record['strip_sha256']):
            raise ValueError('picture frame count changed: ' + str(path))
        for frame, expected in zip(frames, record['strip_sha256']):
            strip = frame.convert('RGB').crop(box)
            if digest(strip.tobytes()) != expected or strip.size != tuple(record['strip_size']):
                raise ValueError('missing burned-in strip: ' + str(path))
            template = strip_for(record, image.size)
            if template.size != strip.size or digest(template.tobytes()) != record['stamp_template_sha256']:
                raise ValueError('invalid stamp bounds: ' + str(path))
            # JPEG and GIF quantize pixels. The text still has to be present.
            from PIL import ImageChops, ImageStat
            if max(ImageStat.Stat(ImageChops.difference(strip, template)).mean) > 18:
                raise ValueError('provenance text is absent from the picture: ' + str(path))
    return record


def open_picture(path, required=False):
    """Restore only the pixels hidden by the strip, for crops and measurements."""
    path = Path(path)
    record = verify(path) if sidecar(path).exists() or required else None
    with Image.open(path) as original:
        image = original.copy()
    if record:
        patch = Image.open(io.BytesIO(base64.b64decode(record['covered_pixels'][0])))

# Drives the regular title, campaign controls and battle input handlers. The rules API is read-only here.
const ShipView = preload("res://render/ship_view.gd")
const PlaceholderGuard = preload("res://render/placeholder_guard.gd")
const Routes := {
	10: {"sector_enemy": "hegemony", "stops": ["0-0", "1-3", "2-2"]},
	121: {"sector_enemy": "corsair", "stops": ["0-0", "1-0", "2-0"]},
}
const FPS := 30

var main: Node
var battle: Node
var route: Dictionary
var report_file: FileAccess
var battle_number := 0
var moved := {}
var last_cut := ""
var danger_seen := false
var first_preview := false
var rewards_seen := {}
var headless := false


func start(game: Node) -> void:
	main = game
	headless = DisplayServer.get_name() == "headless"
	var profile := OS.get_environment("VS_PLAYER_PATH_PROFILE").replace("\\", "/").trim_suffix("/")
	var actual := OS.get_user_data_dir().replace("\\", "/")
	if profile.is_empty() or not actual.to_lower().begins_with(profile.to_lower() + "/") or FileAccess.file_exists("user://settings.json") or FileAccess.file_exists("user://voidsworn_campaign.json") or FileAccess.file_exists("user://battle_opts.json"):
		_fail("start with a fresh isolated player profile from player_path.bat")
		return
	var seed := int(main.args.get("player-path-seed", 0))
	if not Routes.has(seed):
		_fail("unknown seed, expected 10 or 121")
		return
	route = Routes[seed]
	report_file = FileAccess.open(str(main.args["player-path"]), FileAccess.WRITE)
	if report_file == null:
		_fail("could not open the recording log")
		return
	main.show_title()
	await _pause(1.5)
	if not headless and get_viewport().get_texture().get_size() != Vector2(1920, 1080):
		_fail("recording viewport is not 1920 by 1080")
		return
	var build_info = JSON.parse_string(FileAccess.get_file_as_string("res://build_info.json")) if FileAccess.file_exists("res://build_info.json") else null
	_event("run", {"seed": seed, "sector_enemy": route.sector_enemy, "settings": main.settings, "manifest_sha256": FileAccess.get_sha256(ShipView.manifest_path), "mesh_lod_threshold": get_viewport().mesh_lod_threshold, "headless": headless, "build": build_info})
	if not _press(main.title, "New run"):
		_fail("New run button is unavailable")
		return
	await _pause(1.0)
	var steps := 0
	while steps < 70:
		steps += 1
		var host = main.campaign
		if not is_instance_valid(host):
			_fail("the campaign returned to title")
			return
		if is_instance_valid(battle):
			await _play_battle()
			if battle_number == 3 or main.is_queued_for_deletion():
				return
		elif not host.in_battle:
			if not await _campaign_step(host):
				return
		await _pause(0.4)
	_fail("campaign stopped making progress")


func watch_battle(b: Node, api, host: Node) -> void:
	battle = b
	battle_number += 1
	moved.clear()
	danger_seen = false
	first_preview = false
	last_cut = ""
	b.recording_cue.connect(_battle_cue)
	b.arrival.connect(_arrival)
	var cfg: Dictionary = host.cfg_now
	_event("battle_start", {"node": cfg.get("node"), "enemy": cfg.get("fac"), "objective": cfg.get("obj"), "seed": int(cfg.get("seed", 0)), "teaching_battle": cfg.get("teachingBattle", 0)})
	if battle_number == 2 and (cfg.get("obj") != "hold" or not api.teaching_info().get("event") is Dictionary):
		_fail("Battle 2 did not roll the relay and its teaching reinforcement")


func _frame() -> int:
	return Engine.get_process_frames() if headless else Engine.get_frames_drawn()


func _event(kind: String, values := {}) -> void:
	var row := {"kind": kind, "frame": _frame(), "battle": battle_number}
	row.merge(values)
	report_file.store_line(JSON.stringify(row))
	report_file.flush()


func _fail(reason: String) -> void:
	printerr("[player-path] FAIL ", reason)
	if report_file != null:
		_event("failure", {"reason": reason})
		report_file.close()
	main.quit_clean(1)


func _pause(seconds: float) -> void:
	await get_tree().create_timer(seconds).timeout


func _press(node: Node, caption: String) -> bool:
	var button := _button(node, caption)
	if button == null:
		return false
	button.pressed.emit()
	return true


func _button(node: Node, caption: String) -> Button:
	if node == null:
		return null
	for item in node.find_children("*", "Button", true, false):
		var button := item as Button
		if button.text == caption and button.is_visible_in_tree() and not button.disabled:
			return button
	return null


func _campaign_step(host: Node) -> bool:
	var c = host.C
	var ui = host.ui
	var keep_name := _button(host.story, "Keep the name")
	if keep_name != null:
		_event("campaign_card", {"id": "name_flagship"})
		await _pause(3.0)
		keep_name.pressed.emit()
		return true
	if ui.teaching.current != "":
		_event("campaign_card", {"id": ui.teaching.current})
		await _pause(3.0)
		_press(ui.teaching, "Got it")
		await _pause(0.35)
		return true
	var page: String = str(c.page.get("kind", ""))
	var on: Array = c.screen_acts().filter(func(a): return int(a[2]) == 1)
	if page == "map" and c.modal_d == null:
		var id: String = route.stops[battle_number]
		if not on.any(func(a): return a[0] == "enterNode" and a[1] == id):
			_fail("expected route %s is missing after Battle %d" % [id, battle_number])
			return false
		if c.RUN.map.fac != route.sector_enemy:
			_fail("seed did not produce the expected Sector I enemy")
			return false
		ui.pick_route(id)
		await _pause(1.0)
		ui._jump()
		# Normal map mode animates the ship's jump before handing over to the campaign.
		for i in 45:
			if host.in_battle or c.modal_d != null:
				return true
			await _pause(0.2)
		_fail("jump to %s did not open" % id)
		return false
	if page == "end":
		_fail("the run ended before the three battles were recorded")
		return false
	var choice := _campaign_choice(on)
	if choice.is_empty():
		_fail("no supported player choice on %s: %s" % [page, str(on)])
		return false
	_event("campaign_choice", {"page": page, "action": choice[0], "choice": choice[1]})
	await _pause(1.0)
	ui._campaign_action(str(choice[0]), str(choice[1]))
	return true


func _campaign_choice(on: Array) -> Array:
	for name in ["startRun", "pickAspect", "prizeSet", "prizeConfirm", "takeSalvage", "salvageStep", "prizeStep",
		"takeBoon", "takeMod", "takeRelic", "takePerk", "rewardNext", "eventChoice", "closeModal"]:
		for a in on:
			if a[0] != name:
				continue
			if name == "prizeSet" or name == "takeSalvage":
				var pick := str(a[1])
				var key := "%d:%s:%s" % [battle_number, name, pick.get_slice(":", 0)]
				if rewards_seen.has(key) or not (pick.ends_with(":add") or pick.ends_with(":tow") or pick.ends_with(":mod") or pick.ends_with(":cred") or pick.ends_with(":ransom")):
					continue
				rewards_seen[key] = true

"""Make review clips and sheets from the 1080p Godot Movie Maker masters."""
import argparse
import json
import math
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
from datetime import datetime, timezone

from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont

FPS = 30
SECONDS_PER_PART = 48
CLIP_LIMIT = 8_000_000
ROUTES = {10: ("navy", "Navy"), 121: ("hullbreakers", "Hullbreakers")}
INK = "#10151e"
BONE = "#f1e8d9"
EMBER = "#ffae70"
FONT = Path(os.environ.get("WINDIR", r"C:\Windows")) / "Fonts" / "consola.ttf"


def ffmpeg():
    command = os.environ.get("FFMPEG") or shutil.which("ffmpeg")
    if command:
        return command
    import imageio_ffmpeg

    return imageio_ffmpeg.get_ffmpeg_exe()


def run(*args, **options):
    return subprocess.run([ffmpeg(), "-hide_banner", "-nostdin", *map(str, args)], check=True, **options)


def write_json(path, value):
    path.write_text(json.dumps(value, indent=2, ensure_ascii=True) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")


def stamp_rows(provenance):
    return (
        f"Commit {provenance['source_commit']}",
        f"Build SHA-256 {provenance['build_sha256']}",
        f"Manifest SHA-256 {provenance['manifest_sha256']}",
    )


def sidecar(path, provenance, **fields):
    record = dict(provenance, picture_name=path.name, **fields)
    write_json(path.with_name(path.name + ".provenance.json"), record)


def title_for(event):
    kind = event["kind"]
    return {
        "battle_start": "Opening board",
        "board": "Fleet and HUD",
        "danger": "Danger overlay",
        "teaching_card": f"Card / {event.get('id', '').replace('_', ' ')}",
        "move_preview": "Move preview",
        "target_preview": "Target preview",
        "attack_move_preview": "Attack-move preview",
        "cut_start": f"Cut-in / {event.get('text', '').split(':')[0]}",
        "arrival": "Reinforcements",
        "command": "Command",
        "end_card": str(event.get("result", "")).title(),
    }.get(kind, kind)


def contact_times(events, start, stop):
    handed_over = next((event["frame"] / FPS for event in events if event["kind"] == "board"), start + 1.0)
    opening = max(start + 0.3, handed_over - 0.7)
    picks = [(min(opening, stop), "Opening board")]
    priorities = [
        ("teaching_card", "danger"),
        ("move_preview", "attack_move_preview"),
        ("target_preview",),
        ("arrival", "cut_start", "command"),
        ("end_card",),
    ]
    for kinds in priorities:
        pick = next((e for kind in kinds for e in events if e["kind"] == kind), None)
        if pick:
            delay = 0.6 if pick["kind"] in {"arrival", "cut_start", "command"} else 0.27
            picks.append((min(pick["frame"] / FPS + delay, stop), title_for(pick)))
    for fraction in (0.32, 0.52, 0.73, 0.82, 0.92):
        if len(picks) == 6:
            break
        picks.append((start + fraction * (stop - start), "Battle in progress"))
    return sorted(picks)


def frame_image(movie, seconds, width, height):
    pixels = run("-loglevel", "error", "-ss", f"{seconds:.6f}", "-i", movie, "-frames:v", "1",
                 "-vf", f"scale={width}:{height}", "-f", "rawvideo", "-pix_fmt", "rgb24", "-",
                 stdout=subprocess.PIPE).stdout
    if len(pixels) != width * height * 3:
        raise RuntimeError(f"No frame at {seconds:.2f} seconds in {movie}")
    return Image.frombytes("RGB", (width, height), pixels)


def contact_sheet(movie, path, title, events, start, stop, provenance):
    sheet = Image.new("RGB", (1280, 720), INK)
    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(sheet)
    draw.text((25, 21), title, font=ImageFont.truetype(str(FONT), 30), fill=BONE)
    draw.text((25, 57), "Times are measured from the start of this battle.", font=ImageFont.truetype(str(FONT), 17), fill=EMBER)
    labels = []
    for i, (sec, name) in enumerate(contact_times(events, start, stop - 0.1)):
        x, y = 24 + (i % 3) * 416, 95 + (i // 3) * 263
        sheet.paste(frame_image(movie, sec, 400, 225), (x, y))
        offset = sec - start
        minute, second = divmod(offset, 60)
        label = f"{int(minute):02d}:{second:04.1f}  {name}"
        draw.text((x, y + 233), label, font=ImageFont.truetype(str(FONT), 18), fill=BONE)
        labels.append({"seconds": round(offset, 2), "label": name})
    for i, row in enumerate(stamp_rows(provenance)):
        draw.text((25, 647 + 20 * i), row, font=ImageFont.truetype(str(FONT), 17), fill=BONE)
    sheet.save(path, quality=91, optimize=True)
    sidecar(path, provenance, frames=labels)


def review_clips(movie, out, scratch, code, title, start, stop, provenance):
    parts = max(1, math.ceil((stop - start) / SECONDS_PER_PART))
    for index in range(parts):
        elapsed = index * SECONDS_PER_PART
        duration = min(SECONDS_PER_PART, stop - start - elapsed)
        stem = f"{code}-p{index + 1:02d}"
        path = out / f"{stem}.mp4"
        badge = Image.new("RGB", (1280, 72), INK)
        draw = ImageDraw.Draw(badge)
        small = ImageFont.truetype(str(FONT), 14)
        draw.text((16, 4), f"{title} | part {index + 1}/{parts} | {elapsed // 60:02d}:{elapsed % 60:02d}", font=small, fill=EMBER)
        for i, row in enumerate(stamp_rows(provenance)):
            draw.text((16, 19 + i * 16), row, font=small, fill=BONE)
        stamp = scratch / f"{stem}.png"
        badge.save(stamp)
        run("-loglevel", "error", "-n", "-ss", f"{start + elapsed:.6f}", "-i", movie, "-i", stamp,
            "-filter_complex_threads", "2", "-filter_complex",
            "[0:v]scale=1152:648,pad=1280:720:64:0,setsar=1[board];[board][1:v]overlay=0:648[out]",
            "-map", "[out]", "-map", "0:a:0", "-t", f"{duration:.6f}", "-c:v", "libx264",
            "-threads", "4", "-preset", "veryfast", "-b:v", "980k", "-maxrate", "1100k", "-bufsize", "1400k",
            "-pix_fmt", "yuv420p", "-c:a", "aac", "-b:a", "96k", "-movflags", "+faststart",
            "-metadata", f"title={title}, part {index + 1}", "-metadata", "comment=" + " | ".join(stamp_rows(provenance)), path)
        if path.stat().st_size >= CLIP_LIMIT:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Review clip exceeds {CLIP_LIMIT} bytes: {path}")
        sidecar(path, provenance, seconds_in_battle=elapsed, duration_s=round(duration, 3))


def package(full, out, scratch, seed, build_hash):
    movie = full / f"seed-{seed}.avi"
    events = [json.loads(line) for line in (full / f"seed-{seed}.jsonl").read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()]
    setup = next(row for row in events if row["kind"] == "run")
    build = setup.get("build") or {}
    commit = build.get("commit", "")
    manifest = setup.get("manifest_sha256", "")
    if (setup.get("headless") or setup["seed"] != seed or not re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{40,64}", commit)
            or any(not re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]{64}", v) for v in (manifest, build_hash))):
        raise RuntimeError("The capture is missing installed build provenance")
    if (setup["mesh_lod_threshold"] <= 0 or setup["settings"]["cine"] != "key" or not setup["settings"]["tips"]
            or not setup["settings"]["sound"] or setup["settings"]["ai"] != "standard"):
        raise RuntimeError("The capture did not use the player's defaults")
    completed = [row["battle"] for row in events if row["kind"] == "battle_end"]
    for row in (event for event in events if event["kind"] == "board"):
        if not (row["tips_on"] and row["hud"] and row["voice_on"] and row["cut_ins"] == "key"):
            raise RuntimeError(f"Player cues are missing in seed {seed}, Battle {row['battle']}")
    date = datetime.now(timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
    source = {"source_commit": commit, "source_commit_date": build["commit_date"], "stage": "installed in game",
              "source_dirty": build.get("source_dirty", False), "source_diff_sha256": build.get("source_diff_sha256", "")}
    provenance = dict(source, schema=1, date=date, sources=[source], inputs=[], seed=seed, build_sha256=build_hash,
                      manifest_sha256=manifest, fps=FPS)
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    sidecar(movie, provenance, resolution=[1920, 1080])
    route, label = ROUTES[seed]
    for battle in completed:
        rows = [row for row in events if row["battle"] == battle]
        begin = next(row for row in rows if row["kind"] == "battle_start")
        end = next(row for row in rows if row["kind"] == "battle_end")
        start, stop = begin["frame"] / FPS, end["frame"] / FPS
        code = f"{route}-b{battle}"
        title = f"{code.upper()} | {label} route | Seed {seed}"
        one = dict(provenance, battle=battle, enemy=begin["enemy"], objective=begin["objective"])
        review_clips(movie, out, scratch, code, title, start, stop, one)
        contact_sheet(movie, out / f"{code}.jpg", title, rows, start, stop, one)
    if completed != [1, 2, 3] or any(row["kind"] == "failure" for row in events):
        raise RuntimeError(f"Seed {seed} stopped after recording {len(completed)} battles")


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--check", action="store_true")
    parser.add_argument("full", nargs="?", type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("out", nargs="?", type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("scratch", nargs="?", type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("seed", nargs="?", type=int, choices=ROUTES)
    parser.add_argument("build_hash", nargs="?")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.check:
        run("-version", stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL)
        ImageFont.truetype(str(FONT), 17)
    elif None in (args.full, args.out, args.scratch, args.seed, args.build_hash):
        parser.error("provide full, review and scratch folders, seed and build hash")
    else:
        package(args.full, args.out, args.scratch, args.seed, args.build_hash)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

extends Node
# The game's root: title, settings, the campaign (G3, godot/campaign/) and the battles in between, as one game.
# README.md in this folder is the hand-off contract with the campaign: start_battle(run, cfg) returns the result.
#
# Command line (after --):
#   --battle [battle args]   go straight into a quick battle (scenes/battle/battle.gd takes the same args)
#   --bot-runs N             headless test: N full runs (campaign runs when the campaign is in, otherwise sector 1
#                            battles) with both sides on AI; prints one JSON line per run and a summary, and writes
#                            --report <file> (JSON). --fast K speeds the clock K times (default 40).

const BattleScene := preload("res://scenes/battle/battle.tscn")
const Title := preload("res://main/title.gd")
const Host := preload("res://main/campaign_host.gd")
const CAMPAIGN_SCRIPT := "res://campaign/campaign.gd"

signal battle_done(result: Dictionary)

var title: CanvasLayer
